CarePoint HMS
Endpoint Utilization Guide
Frontend sequencing, multi-tenant authentication model, and complete inventory of every public REST endpoint with request and response payloads.
Base URL
http://localhost:8005
API Prefix
/api/v1
Tenant routing
Subdomain (acme.carepointhms.com), X-Tenant-Code header, X-Tenant-Domain header, custom domain, or JWT tenant_id claim.
Tenant user auth
Authorization: Bearer <access_token> (tenant-scoped JWT)
SaaS admin auth
Authorization: Bearer <access_token> (with is_saas_admin=true claim)
Patient portal auth
OTP-issued bearer token via /auth/portal/* flow
Public endpoints
/auth/login, /auth/refresh, /auth/forgot-password, /invitations/accept, /tenants/register, paystack webhook
Total endpoints documented
759

1. Platform overview
CarePoint HMS is a multi-tenant SaaS hospital management platform. The API is exposed at /api/v1 and supports four authentication contexts: tenant users, SaaS administrators, patients (portal), and public callers (auth/registration/webhooks).
Frontend implementations should be sequenced in layers: SaaS admin and platform setup first, tenant onboarding next, then the tenant's own configuration (settings, email, payment methods), then authentication and RBAC, then operational HMS workflows, and finally reporting and dashboards. The phases below mirror that order.
Audience
How to authenticate
Tenant user
Bearer JWT (tenant_id + tenant_code claims). Routed to the tenant's own database.
SaaS administrator
Bearer JWT with is_saas_admin=true. Operates against the master database; tenant data requires an active SupportAccessGrant.
Patient
OTP-issued JWT for the patient portal. Scoped to a single tenant via the resolved subdomain or X-Tenant-Code header.
Public
Auth, refresh, password reset, invitation acceptance, registration, and webhook callbacks.

2. Frontend implementation order at a glance
Phase
Frontend responsibility
Why it comes here
Phase 1
Platform setup (SaaS admins, plans, dashboards, edge nodes).
Required before any tenant can be onboarded.
Phase 2
Tenant onboarding & approval (registration, billing, domains, modules).
An onboarded tenant is the prerequisite for everything tenant-scoped.
Phase 3
Tenant configuration (settings, email, payment methods, modules, jobs, backups).
Tenants set up their environment before inviting users.
Phase 4
Authentication & RBAC (auth, roles, permissions, users).
Required before any operational endpoint can be exercised.
Phase 5
Organisation & staffing (facilities, departments, wards, beds, staff, HR, shifts, payroll).
Reference data clinical workflows depend on.
Phase 6
Patient identity & engagement (registration, MPI, portal, membership cards, history).
No clinical activity is meaningful without a patient record.
Phase 7
Visit, queue & triage (visit creation, flow, queue, triage, vitals, appointments).
Front-of-house operations and OPD entry.
Phase 8
Clinical care (consultations, diagnoses, referrals).
Doctor-facing workflows that happen during a visit.
Phase 9
Laboratory (catalog, orders, results).
Activated when a clinician orders investigations.
Phase 10
Pharmacy & inventory (drugs, prescriptions, dispenses, inventory, stock).
Activated for prescription fulfilment and stock control.
Phase 11
Billing, payments & insurance (billing, invoices, payments, claims).
Activated once chargeable services are produced.
Phase 12
Inpatient operations (admissions, discharges).
Specialised workflows for admitted patients.
Phase 13
Procedures, radiology & surgical.
Optional modules gated by subscription plan.
Phase 14
Reports, ambulance, compliance & ops.
Best implemented after transactional flows produce data.

3. How to read the endpoint tables
Each endpoint table has five columns:
• Method — HTTP verb (GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE).
• Endpoint — full path under /api/v1. Path parameters are written {snake_case}.
• Description — what the endpoint does.
• Request Body — example JSON shape the client must send (or — when the endpoint takes no body).
• Response — example JSON shape returned on success. List endpoints render an array (e.g. [ {...} ]).
JSON conventions used in the tables: literal example values stand in for types (e.g. "string", 0, true, "2025-01-01T00:00:00Z"). Inline comments mark optional fields (// optional) and nullable values (// nullable). Schemas with more than 30 fields are truncated; full definitions live under app/schemas/.
All secured endpoints expect Authorization: Bearer <access_token>. Tenant routing is resolved before authentication via subdomain, X-Tenant-Code header or custom domain.

4. Complete endpoint inventory
All 759 endpoints currently exposed under /api/v1, grouped by phase and tag. Authentication rules follow the audience matrix in section 1.

/api/v1/approvals/flows
Create a new approval flow with steps and approvers
{
  "code": "string",
  "name": "string",
  "description": "string",  // optional, nullable
  "subject_type": "<ApprovalSubjectType>",
  "is_default": true,  // optional
  "sla_hours": 0,  // optional, nullable
  "auto_cancel_after_hours": 0,  // optional, nullable
  "notify_on_submit": true,  // optional
  "notify_on_decision": true,  // optional
  "steps": [
    {
      "step_order": 0,
      "name": "string",
      "description": "string",  // optional, nullable
      "decision_rule": "<ApprovalStepDecisionRule>",  // optional
      "required_approvals": 0,  // optional
      "allow_self_approval": true,  // optional
      "sla_hours": 0,  // optional, nullable
      "is_optional": true,  // optional
      "parallel_group": "string",  // optional, nullable
      "condition": { "<key>": "any" },  // nullable
      "approvers": ["<ApprovalFlowStepApproverCreateSchema (object)>"]  // optional
    }
  ]
}
{
  "success": true,  // optional
  "message": "string",
  "flow": {
    "id": 0,

Connectivity

Endpoint
Description
Request Body
Response

/api/v1/connectivity/probe
Lightweight connectivity probe for offline-aware clients
—
—

5. Frontend implementation checklist
Always send Authorization: Bearer <access_token> on secured requests.
Resolve the tenant by subdomain in production; fall back to X-Tenant-Code header in development tools.
Refresh access tokens proactively when ~1 minute remains before expiry.
Surface two_factor_required=true from /auth/login and route to the OTP screen.
On 401, attempt a refresh once; on a second 401, clear the session and bounce to login.
On 403 with detail.module=<name>, surface a 'module not enabled in your subscription' notice.
Capture invoice number/receipt number returned by patient-payment endpoints; they appear on the receipt email.
Render the response detail object verbatim on form errors — every endpoint returns structured error metadata.
Cache /tenant-payment-methods, /settings, and /roles for the duration of a session; invalidate on settings save.
All file-upload endpoints (logo, profile photo, KYC, lab results) are multipart/form-data; respect the configured maximum_amount and max-file-size limits.
